# 📖 StackCalc Master Answer Key & Keystroke Verification Portal

> **The Paper-First Principle:** Calculators should never replace reasoning. Students first build the problem with physical 3D blocks or write their mathematical setup, solve on paper by hand, and verify on their distraction-free StackCalc instrument. This portal provides complete worked solutions and canonical keystrokes across all volumes in *The StackCalc Library*.

## 📗 Book 2A: Early Elementary Math Lab (Grades K–2 • SC-6)
*Covers Units 01 through 10 plus 4 Cumulative Benchmark & Capstone Mastery Exams: Step Counting, Number Bonds, Make-10 Trades, Doubling, Place Value, Subtraction, Regrouping Carry, Two-Digit Operations, Equal Groups & Arrays, and Measurement/Money (840 active problems).*

### Unit 01: Counting, Cardinality & The One-More Step Counter
**Grades:** Pre-K – Kindergarten | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (Digit Blocks 0–9 & Gold [+1] Step Tokens)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.K.CC.A.1, CCSS.MATH.K.CC.B.4, CCSS.MATH.K.OA.A.1

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Place the [4] Digit Block in the Ones column of your Place Value Tray and add 1 Gold [+1] step token. What total quantity do you have now?
  - *Verified Answer:* `5 ([5] Digit Block; 4 + 1 = 5).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 1 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Row 1 holds [6] and Row 0 holds [2] on your Place Value Tray. Which row has more, and how many more does it have?
  - *Verified Answer:* `Row 1 has 4 more (6 - 2 = 4).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `6 ENTER 2 -`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Place [3] in the top row and [2] in the bottom row of your Place Value Tray. Combine both groups—what is the total? | **5** | `3 ENTER 2 +` |
| 2 | You have 5 ones on the tray and take away 2. What Digit Block shows how many ones remain? | **3** | `5 ENTER 2 -` |
| 3 | You have 3 blocks on the tray and take away all 3. How many blocks are left, and which Digit Block shows an empty tray? | **0 (Zero)** | `3 ENTER 3 -` |
| 4 | Start with [1] in the Ones socket and add one more [+1] token three times in a row. What count do you reach? | **4** | `1 ENTER 1 + 1 + 1 +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Garden Stepping Stone Trail
> *Scenario:* A landscape gardener designs a walking path with stone markers spaced 1 meter apart. Students calculate steps, distances, and total stones needed.

- **Case Question 1:** Start at Stone 4 and lay 3 more stones. What stone number are you on?
  - *Expected Result:* **7** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 1 + 1 + 1 +`
- **Case Question 2:** A trail has 8 stones. A bird lands on stone 5. How many stones remain to the end?
  - *Expected Result:* **3** | *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 5 -`
- **Case Question 3:** Two parallel paths have 4 stones and 5 stones. How many stones total?
  - *Expected Result:* **9** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 5 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Leo says stepping forward 2 stones from 6 gives stone 9. Find the true stone.
  - *Expected Result:* **Stone 8 (6 + 2 = 8, not 9).** | *Keystrokes:* `6 ENTER 2 +`

### Unit 02: Part-Part-Whole & Number Bonds on the Place Value Tray
**Grades:** Kindergarten – Grade 1 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (Number Bond Stage & Digit Blocks 0–9)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.K.OA.A.3, CCSS.MATH.1.OA.B.3, CCSS.MATH.1.OA.C.6

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Place [5] in Row 1 (Part 1) and [4] in Row 0 (Part 2) of your Place Value Tray. Combine the two parts to find the Whole.
  - *Verified Answer:* `9 ([9] Digit Block; 5 + 4 = 9).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `5 ENTER 4 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** A number bond has a Whole of 8 and a known Part of 5. Find the missing Part 2.
  - *Verified Answer:* `Missing Part is 3 (8 - 5 = 3).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 5 -`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | To make a Whole of 6, Part 1 is 2. What is the missing Part 2? | **4** | `6 ENTER 2 -` |
| 2 | In a number bond, Part 1 is 7 and Part 2 is 2. What is the Whole when you combine both parts? | **9** | `7 ENTER 2 +` |
| 3 | Three parts join together: first combine 2 and 3, then add 4 to that sum. What is the Whole? | **9** | `2 ENTER 3 + 4 +` |
| 4 | Ten birds sit on a fence. Seven fly to a tree. How many stay on the fence? | **3** | `1 0 ENTER 7 -` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Space Station Cargo Balancing Tray
> *Scenario:* Astronauts balance payloads on the supply shuttle. Each cargo tray holds exactly 10 kg across two compartments (Part 1 and Part 2).

- **Case Question 1:** Compartment A holds 6 kg. How many kg fit into Compartment B to make 10 kg?
  - *Expected Result:* **4 kg** | *Keystrokes:* `10 ENTER 6 -`
- **Case Question 2:** Compartment A holds 3 kg. What is the missing cargo weight for a 10 kg tray?
  - *Expected Result:* **7 kg** | *Keystrokes:* `10 ENTER 3 -`
- **Case Question 3:** Two small containers weigh 4 kg and 5 kg. Can they both fit in a 10 kg tray?
  - *Expected Result:* **Yes, 9 kg total (1 kg space left).** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 5 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Sam says 10 − 8 = 3. Use the number bond to find the correct missing part.
  - *Expected Result:* **2 kg (8 + 2 = 10, not 3).** | *Keystrokes:* `10 ENTER 8 -`

### Unit 03: Complements to 10 & Make-10 Regrouping with Carry Tokens
**Grades:** Grade 1 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (Ten-Frame Trays & Gold [+1] Carry Tokens)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.1.OA.C.6, CCSS.MATH.1.NBT.B.2, CCSS.MATH.2.OA.B.2

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** The Ones socket holds [6]. How many more ones are needed to complete a full 10 and trade for 1 Ten?
  - *Verified Answer:* `4 ones (10 - 6 = 4).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `1 0 ENTER 6 -`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Use the Make-Ten strategy on your Place Value Tray to add 9 + 6. What is the sum?
  - *Verified Answer:* `15 (9 + 1 = 10, then 10 + 5 = 15).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `9 ENTER 1 + 5 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | You have 4 ones on your tray. How many more ones do you need to make a full 10? | **6** | `1 0 ENTER 4 -` |
| 2 | Solve 7 + 5 by bridging through 10 on your Place Value Tray. | **12** | `7 ENTER 3 + 2 +` |
| 3 | The Ones socket holds [9]. How many more ones are needed to complete 10 and regroup into 1 Ten? | **1** | `1 0 ENTER 9 -` |
| 4 | Solve 8 + 6 by bridging through 10 on your Place Value Tray. | **14** | `8 ENTER 2 + 4 +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Farm Fresh Egg Carton Packaging
> *Scenario:* A poultry farm packages organic eggs into cartons of 10. When a carton fills, workers close the lid and pack a new carton.

- **Case Question 1:** A basket has 8 brown eggs and 5 white eggs. How many full 10-cartons and loose eggs?
  - *Expected Result:* **1 full carton of 10 and 3 loose eggs (13 eggs).** | *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 5 +`
- **Case Question 2:** A carton has 7 eggs. How many more eggs are needed to fill the 10-frame carton?
  - *Expected Result:* **3 eggs** | *Keystrokes:* `10 ENTER 7 -`
- **Case Question 3:** Two nests have 9 eggs and 6 eggs. What is the total egg harvest?
  - *Expected Result:* **15 eggs (1 ten and 5 ones).** | *Keystrokes:* `9 ENTER 6 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A student writes 8 + 6 = 15. Count the dots to find the true sum.
  - *Expected Result:* **14 eggs (8 + 2 = 10, 10 + 4 = 14).** | *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 6 +`

### Unit 04: Doubles, Near-Doubles & Addition Facts within 20
**Grades:** Grade 1 – 2 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (Twin-Row Doubling Mat & Digit Blocks)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.1.OA.C.6, CCSS.MATH.2.OA.B.2

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Place [7] in both rows of your Place Value Tray to build a twin-row double. What is the sum of two 7s?
  - *Verified Answer:* `14 ([1] Ten and [4] Ones on the tray; 7 + 7 = 14).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `7 ENTER +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Use the known double 6 + 6 to solve the near-double 6 + 7.
  - *Verified Answer:* `13 (6 + 6 = 12, then 12 + 1 = 13).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `6 ENTER + 1 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Two matching rows on the Place Value Tray each hold 8. What is double 8? | **16** | `8 ENTER +` |
| 2 | Use the double 8 + 8 to solve the near-double 8 + 9. | **17** | `8 ENTER + 1 +` |
| 3 | A carton has two equal rows of 9 eggs. How many eggs are in the carton? | **18** | `9 ENTER +` |
| 4 | Use the double 5 + 5 to solve the near-double 5 + 6. | **11** | `5 ENTER + 1 +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The School Bus Morning Seating Chart
> *Scenario:* A school bus has two identical columns of seats. Children sit in pairs: one on the window side, one on the aisle side.

- **Case Question 1:** Row 1 through 7 each have 2 students (7 on left, 7 on right). How many students total?
  - *Expected Result:* **14 students (7 + 7 = 14).** | *Keystrokes:* `7 ENTER +`
- **Case Question 2:** 8 students sit on the left and 9 on the right. Use near-doubles to find total passengers.
  - *Expected Result:* **17 students ((8 + 8) + 1 = 17).** | *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER + 1 +`
- **Case Question 3:** 6 boys and 6 girls sit in the front section. How many students in the front section?
  - *Expected Result:* **12 students** | *Keystrokes:* `6 ENTER +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Mia doubles 9 and gets 17. Use the stack to find double 9.
  - *Expected Result:* **18 (9 [ENTER] [+] = 18).** | *Keystrokes:* `9 ENTER +`

### Unit 05: Two-Digit Place Value: Tens & Ones on the Place Value Tray
**Grades:** Grade 1 – 2 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (6×2 Whole Number Tray & Digit Blocks 0–9)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.1.NBT.B.2, CCSS.MATH.2.NBT.A.1

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Build 47 on your Place Value Tray. Add 30. What is the sum?
  - *Verified Answer:* `77 (7 tens and 7 ones).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `47 ENTER 30 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Start with 82 on your Place Value Tray. Subtract 40. What number remains?
  - *Verified Answer:* `42 (4 tens and 2 ones).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `82 ENTER 40 -`

| 3 | Build 41 on your Place Value Tray. Add 40: 41 + 40 = | **81** | `41 ENTER 40 +` |
| 4 | Build 93 on your Place Value Tray. Subtract 50: 93 − 50 = | **43** | `93 ENTER 50 -` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Classroom Library Book Cart
> *Scenario:* Student librarians organize books onto a rolling cart. Each large bin holds exactly 10 books (1 Ten), and the top rack holds single display books (Ones).

- **Case Question 1:** Cart A has 4 full bins of 10 books and 7 display books. How many books are on Cart A?
  - *Expected Result:* **47 books** | *Keystrokes:* `40 ENTER 7 +`
- **Case Question 2:** 2 full bins of 10 books are added to Cart A. How many books are on the cart now?
  - *Expected Result:* **67 books** | *Keystrokes:* `47 ENTER 20 +`
- **Case Question 3:** Cart B starts with 86 books. Classrooms check out 3 full bins of 10 books. How many books remain?
  - *Expected Result:* **56 books** | *Keystrokes:* `86 ENTER 30 -`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Mia counts 5 bins of 10 and 3 single books and writes 35. Find the true total!
  - *Expected Result:* **53 books (50 + 3 = 53, not 35).** | *Keystrokes:* `50 ENTER 3 +`

### Unit 06: Subtraction as Take-Away & Comparison on the Tray
**Grades:** Grade 1 – 2 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (2-Row Tray, Digit Blocks & Gold [+1] Tokens)

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Place [1][6] in Row 1 of your Place Value Tray and [9] in Row 0. What is 16 − 9?
  - *Verified Answer:* `7.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `16 ENTER 9 -`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Row 1 holds [1][7] and Row 0 holds [1][2] on your Place Value Tray. Find the difference.
  - *Verified Answer:* `5.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `17 ENTER 12 -`

| 4 | Find the difference between 20 and 14 on the tray: 20 − 14 = | **6** | `20 ENTER 14 -` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Frog Jump Number Line Olympics
> *Scenario:* Biologists track tree frogs hopping along a 20-meter measuring tape beside a pond. Students use subtraction to find jump distances and remaining track lengths.

- **Case Question 1:** Frog A starts at meter 6 and lands at meter 15. How long was its jump?
  - *Expected Result:* **9 meters** | *Keystrokes:* `15 ENTER 6 -`
- **Case Question 2:** Frog B is at meter 13. How many meters left to reach the finish line at meter 20?
  - *Expected Result:* **7 meters** | *Keystrokes:* `20 ENTER 13 -`
- **Case Question 3:** Frog C jumps from 0 to 18, then hops backward 7 meters. What marker is it on now?
  - *Expected Result:* **Meter 11** | *Keystrokes:* `18 ENTER 7 -`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Leo subtracts 16 − 9 and gets 8. Use the number line to find the true distance.
  - *Expected Result:* **7 meters (9 + 7 = 16, not 8).** | *Keystrokes:* `16 ENTER 9 -`

### Unit 07: Make-10 Regrouping with Gold [+1] Carry Tokens
**Grades:** Grade 1 – 2 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (2-Row Tray & Gold [+1] Carry Tokens)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.1.NBT.C.4, CCSS.MATH.2.NBT.B.5

- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Build 37 ([3][7]) on your Place Value Tray and add [6] in Ones. Snap a Gold [+1] Carry Token onto Tens. What is 37 + 6?
  - *Verified Answer:* `43.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `37 ENTER 6 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Add 58 + 25 on your Place Value Tray using a Gold [+1] Carry Token. What is the sum?
  - *Verified Answer:* `83.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `58 ENTER 25 +`

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Coin Bank Regrouping Vault
> *Scenario:* An automated coin sorting machine collects pennies in the Ones chute. Whenever 10 pennies accumulate, it trades them for 1 Dime (10¢) in the Tens chute.

- **Case Question 1:** The vault holds 37¢ (3 dimes, 7 pennies) and 8 more pennies drop in. What is the new total?
  - *Expected Result:* **45¢ (4 dimes, 5 pennies).** | *Keystrokes:* `37 ENTER 8 +`
- **Case Question 2:** Deposit A is 46¢ and Deposit B is 27¢. Combine them and state total cents:
  - *Expected Result:* **73¢** | *Keystrokes:* `46 ENTER 27 +`
- **Case Question 3:** Three coin rolls hold 25¢, 38¢, and 17¢. What is the total value in the vault?
  - *Expected Result:* **80¢** | *Keystrokes:* `25 ENTER 38 + 17 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A clerk adds 58¢ + 26¢ and writes 74¢. Find the true sum with the carry token!
  - *Expected Result:* **84¢ (8 + 6 = 14¢, so 50 + 20 + 14 = 84¢).** | *Keystrokes:* `58 ENTER 26 +`

### Unit 08: Two-Digit Addition & Subtraction (0–99)
**Grades:** Grade 2 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (2-Row Tray, Digit Blocks & [+1] Tokens)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.2.NBT.B.5, CCSS.MATH.2.NBT.B.7

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Solve 45 + 39 on your Place Value Tray and paper first, then verify on your SC-6.
  - *Verified Answer:* `84.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `45 ENTER 39 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Place [6][4] in Row 1 and [2][8] in Row 0 of your Place Value Tray. Unbundle 1 Ten to solve 64 − 28.
  - *Verified Answer:* `36.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `64 ENTER 28 -`

| 3 | Solve 54 + 29 on paper & Place Value Tray, then verify: 54 + 29 = | **83** | `54 ENTER 29 +` |
| 4 | Solve 83 − 47 on paper with unbundling: 83 − 47 = | **36** | `83 ENTER 47 -` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Weather Station Thermometer Log
> *Scenario:* Junior meteorologists record daily high and low temperatures (in °F) and monthly rainfall totals at the school rooftop weather station.

- **Case Question 1:** Monday's high was 74°F and the overnight low was 48°F. What was the temperature swing?
  - *Expected Result:* **26°F** | *Keystrokes:* `74 ENTER 48 -`
- **Case Question 2:** Morning temperature was 56°F and rose 27°F by afternoon. What was the afternoon high?
  - *Expected Result:* **83°F** | *Keystrokes:* `56 ENTER 27 +`
- **Case Question 3:** Rainfall was 38 mm in April and 45 mm in May. How much short of 100 mm is the 2-month total?
  - *Expected Result:* **17 mm** | *Keystrokes:* `100 ENTER 38 ENTER 45 + -`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A student computes 82°F − 47°F = 45°F by doing 7 − 2 = 5. Correct the reading!
  - *Expected Result:* **35°F (unbundle 1 ten: 12 − 7 = 5, 70 − 40 = 30 -> 35°F).** | *Keystrokes:* `82 ENTER 47 -`

### Unit 09: Equal Groups, Repeated Addition & Arrays
**Grades:** Grade 2 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (2-Row Tray & Equal-Group Digit Blocks)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.2.OA.C.3, CCSS.MATH.2.OA.C.4

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Place four [5] Digit Blocks on your Place Value Tray (4 groups of 5). What is 5 + 5 + 5 + 5?
  - *Verified Answer:* `20 (4 groups of 5).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `5 ENTER 5 + 5 + 5 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Place [9] in Row 1 and [9] in Row 0 of your Place Value Tray ([9] [ENTER] [+]). What even number is formed?
  - *Verified Answer:* `18 (Even).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `9 ENTER +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Place three [5] blocks on the tray (3 groups of 5): 5 + 5 + 5 = | **15** | `5 ENTER 5 + 5 +` |
| 2 | Place four [3] blocks on the tray (4 groups of 3): 3 + 3 + 3 + 3 = | **12** | `3 ENTER 3 + 3 + 3 +` |
| 3 | Double [8] on the tray using the instant shortcut: 8 [ENTER] + = | **16** | `8 ENTER +` |
| 4 | Place [9] in Row 1 and [9] in Row 0 of the tray: 9 + 9 = | **18** | `9 ENTER +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Community Garden Veggie Patch
> *Scenario:* Second-grade botanists lay out rectangular raised beds in the school garden, spacing vegetable seedlings in equal rows and columns.

- **Case Question 1:** Bed A has 4 rows of 5 carrot plants. How many carrot plants are in Bed A?
  - *Expected Result:* **20 plants** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 5 ×`
- **Case Question 2:** Bed B has 3 rows of 6 tomato plants. How many tomato plants are in Bed B?
  - *Expected Result:* **18 plants** | *Keystrokes:* `3 ENTER 6 ×`
- **Case Question 3:** Combine Bed A and Bed B. How many vegetable plants are in both beds?
  - *Expected Result:* **38 plants** | *Keystrokes:* `20 ENTER 18 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A student plants 4 rows of 6 sunflowers and writes 4 + 6 = 10. Find the true count!
  - *Expected Result:* **24 sunflowers (6 + 6 + 6 + 6 = 24).** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 6 ×`

### Unit 10: Measurement, Length & Coin Money Pipelines
**Grades:** Grade 2 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (2-Row Tray, Digit Blocks & [+1] Carry Tokens)

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** A ribbon is 28 cm long in Row 1. You cut off 12 cm in Row 0. How much ribbon remains?
  - *Verified Answer:* `16 cm.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `28 ENTER 12 -`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** You buy a pencil for 35¢ and an eraser for 25¢. How much change do you receive from $1.00?
  - *Verified Answer:* `40¢.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `100 ENTER 35 ENTER 25 + -`

| 3 | You spend 72¢ at the store. What is your change from $1.00? | **28¢** | `100 ENTER 72 -` |
| 4 | Combine lengths: side A is 18 cm and side B is 24 cm. Find the total length: | **42 cm** | `18 ENTER 24 +` |

  - *Expected Result:* **46 cm** | *Keystrokes:* `28 ENTER 18 +`
- **Case Question 2:** You cut the 46 cm needed from an 80 cm balsa strip. How many cm of wood are left over?
  - *Expected Result:* **34 cm** | *Keystrokes:* `80 ENTER 46 -`
- **Case Question 3:** A customer buys a glider for 45¢ and a launcher for 30¢ with $1.00. Find their change:

- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A student measures a wing from the 3 cm mark to 15 cm and says 15 cm. Correct it!
  - *Expected Result:* **12 cm (15 − 3 = 12 cm).** | *Keystrokes:* `15 ENTER 3 -`

## 📘 Book 2B: Upper Elementary Math Lab (Grades 3–5 • SC-6)
*Covers Units 01 through 10 plus 4 Cumulative Benchmark & Capstone Mastery Exams: Multiplication Arrays, Division & Remainders, Unit Fractions, Multi-Digit Operations, Long Division & Factors, Fraction Equivalence, Prime Factorization/GCF/LCM, 4-Register Stack PEMDAS, Mixed Numbers, and Fraction Multiplication & Division (840 active problems).*

### Unit 01: Multiplication Concepts, Commutativity & Area Models
**Grades:** Grade 3 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Rectangular Area Grid Diagram & Place Value Studio
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.3.OA.A.1, CCSS.MATH.3.OA.B.5

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** A garden bed is 7 feet long by 9 feet wide. What is its total square footage?
  - *Verified Answer:* `63 sq ft.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `7 ENTER 9 ×`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Solve 6 × 40 using place value tens on paper and verify on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `240 (24 tens).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `6 ENTER 40 ×`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A solar array has 7 rows of 8 panels. How many panels in all? | **56** | `7 ENTER 8 ×` |
| 2 | Multiply a single digit by a multiple of 10: 5 × 40 = | **200** | `5 ENTER 40 ×` |
| 3 | A storage rack holds 9 shelves with 6 bins each. How many bins in all? | **54** | `9 ENTER 6 ×` |
| 4 | Solve 8 × 30 using place value scaling: 8 × 30 = | **240** | `8 ENTER 30 ×` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Warehouse Pallet Storage System
> *Scenario:* Logistics managers calculate warehouse floor space in square meters using area models.

- **Case Question 1:** A pallet bay holds 6 rows of 8 pallets. How many pallets fit in the bay?
  - *Expected Result:* **48** | *Keystrokes:* `6 ENTER 8 ×`
- **Case Question 2:** If 4 bays each have 30 boxes, how many boxes total?
  - *Expected Result:* **120** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 30 ×`
- **Case Question 3:** Rotate a 7 by 9 pallet layout by 90 degrees. Does the area change?
  - *Expected Result:* **No, 63 pallets either way.** | *Keystrokes:* `7 ENTER 9 ×`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A clerk calculates 6 × 80 = 48. Find the true product.
  - *Expected Result:* **480 (6 × 8 tens = 48 tens = 480).** | *Keystrokes:* `6 ENTER 80 ×`

### Unit 02: Division as Fair-Sharing, Grouping & Remainders
**Grades:** Grade 3 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio & Prime Factor Studio
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.3.OA.A.2, CCSS.MATH.3.OA.B.6

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** 36 apples are packed into bags of 4. How many bags can be filled?
  - *Verified Answer:* `9 bags.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `36 ENTER 4 ÷`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** A baker puts 29 cookies into boxes of 6. How many full boxes and how many cookies left over?
  - *Verified Answer:* `4 full boxes, 5 leftover cookies (29 = 6 × 4 + 5).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `29 ENTER 6 ÷R`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Divide 35 candies equally into 5 treat bags. How many per bag? | **7** | `35 ENTER 5 ÷` |
| 2 | How many groups of 8 are in 48? | **6** | `48 ENTER 8 ÷` |
| 3 | Share 27 marbles among 4 friends. Find the quotient and remainder: | **6 R 3** | `27 ENTER 4 ÷R` |
| 4 | Divide 56 books equally across 7 classroom shelves. How many per shelf? | **8** | `56 ENTER 7 ÷` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Wildlife Rescue Meal Distribution
> *Scenario:* Marine biologists divide daily fish rations and vitamin packs among orphaned seal pups and sea otters.

- **Case Question 1:** Share 36 kg of herring equally among 4 seal pups. How many kg per pup?
  - *Expected Result:* **9 kg each** | *Keystrokes:* `36 ENTER 4 ÷`
- **Case Question 2:** Divide 45 vitamin tablets into packs of 6. Find quotient and remainder.
  - *Expected Result:* **7 R 3 (6×7+3=45).** | *Keystrokes:* `45 ENTER 6 ÷R`
- **Case Question 3:** If 54 fish are shared among 9 otters, how many fish does each otter receive?
  - *Expected Result:* **6 fish** | *Keystrokes:* `54 ENTER 9 ÷`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Sam enters 6 [ENTER] 30 [÷] and gets 0.2. What should he do?
  - *Expected Result:* **Enter dividend first: 30 [ENTER] 6 [÷] = 5.** | *Keystrokes:* `30 ENTER 6 ÷`

### Unit 03: Unit Fractions, Denominator Strips & Comparator Heights
**Grades:** Grade 3 – 4 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Fraction Studio (84 mm Proportional Towers)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.3.NF.A.1, CCSS.MATH.3.NF.A.2

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Place two 1/4 blocks into the comparator. What fraction height do they match?
  - *Verified Answer:* `2/4 = 1/2 (42.0 mm).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 4 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Compare 1/4 and 1/8. How many 1/8 blocks equal 1/4?
  - *Verified Answer:* `2 eighths equal 1/4 (21.0 mm).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 8 ENTER . 1 . 8 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Combine two 1/3 fraction blocks: 1/3 + 1/3 = | **2/3** | `. 1 . 3 ENTER . 1 . 3 +` |
| 2 | Subtract 1/4 from 1/2: 1/2 − 1/4 = | **1/4** | `. 1 . 2 ENTER . 1 . 4 -` |
| 3 | Find the height of three 1/6 blocks: 1/6 + 1/6 + 1/6 = | **1/2** | `. 1 . 6 ENTER . 1 . 6 + . 1 . 6 +` |
| 4 | Subtract 1/8 from 1/4: 1/4 − 1/8 = | **1/8** | `. 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 8 -` |

#### 4. 100-Point Unit Mastery Check
| # | Category | Assessment Prompt | Points | Expected Solution | Verification Keys |
|:---:|---|---|:---:|---|---|
| **1** | Core Fluency | Add unit fractions and simplify: 1/4 + 1/4 = | 10 pts | **1/2** | `.1.4 ENTER .1.4 +` |
| **2** | Core Fluency | Subtract like fractions and simplify: 5/6 − 1/6 = | 10 pts | **2/3** | `.5.6 ENTER .1.6 -` |
| **3** | Core Fluency | Find the comparator difference: 1/3 − 1/4 = | 10 pts | **1/12** | `.1.3 ENTER .1.4 -` |
| **4** | Core Fluency | Find the comparator difference: 1/2 − 1/6 = | 10 pts | **1/3** | `.1.2 ENTER .1.6 -` |
| **5** | Core Fluency | Find the complement to 1 Whole: 1 − 3/8 = | 10 pts | **5/8** | `1 ENTER .3.8 -` |
| **6** | Core Fluency | Evaluate chained sum: 1/6 + 1/6 + 1/6 = | 10 pts | **1/2** | `.1.6 ENTER .1.6 + .1.6 +` |
| **7** | Application | If 1 Whole is 84 mm tall, how tall is a 1/3 block in mm? | 10 pts | **28 mm** | `84 ENTER 3 ÷` |
| **8** | Application | How many 1/8 blocks are needed to match 3/4? | 10 pts | **6** | `.3.4 ENTER .1.8 ÷` |
| **9** | Application | A carpenter glues a 3/8 in strip to a 1/8 in strip. Total thickness? | 10 pts | **1/2 in** | `.3.8 ENTER .1.8 +` |
| **10** | Error Analysis | Error Analysis: Noah added 1/4 + 1/4 = 2/8. Simplify 1/4 + 1/4 correctly: | 10 pts | **1/2** | `.1.4 ENTER .1.4 +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Architectural Glass Window Panes
> *Scenario:* An architect proportions structural glass windows using unit fraction ratios.

- **Case Question 1:** Clerestory vs. Transom Pane: An upper clerestory pane is 1/3 of the frame height and a transom pane is 1/4. Compare your 84 mm towers to find the fraction difference:
  - *Expected Result:* **7 mm = 1/12** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 3 ENTER . 1 . 4 -`
- **Case Question 2:** Modular Louver Stack: Each glass louver vent is 1/6 of a meter tall. How many 1/6 louver blocks stack up in the comparator well to fill a 1/2-meter window opening?
  - *Expected Result:* **3 sixths (14×3 = 42 mm = 1/2).** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 2 ENTER . 1 . 6 ÷`
- **Case Question 3:** Compound Mullion Frame: The architect stacks a 1/4-meter tinted pane above a 1/8-meter acoustic sill. What total fraction of a meter is the combined assembly?
  - *Expected Result:* **3/8 (31.5 mm).** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 8 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A junior drafter claims a 1/8-meter glass strip is taller than a 1/4-meter strip because 8 > 4. Use the 84 mm Fraction Comparator to explain why this is false:
  - *Expected Result:* **Larger denominator means smaller pieces: 1/4 (21 mm) > 1/8 (10.5 mm).** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 8 -`

### Unit 04: Multi-Digit Multiplication & Partial Products
**Grades:** Grade 4 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Partial-Product Area Grid Diagram & Place Value Studio
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.4.NBT.B.5

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Use partial products to find 38 × 7 on paper, then check on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `266 ((30×7) + (8×7) = 210 + 56 = 266).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `38 ENTER 7 ×`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Multiply 43 × 26 using an area grid model on paper, then check on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `1118 ((40×20)+(40×6)+(3×20)+(3×6) = 800+240+60+18 = 1118).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `43 ENTER 26 ×`

| 3 | Multiply using area decomposition: 64 × 7 = | **448** | `64 ENTER 7 ×` |
| 4 | Multiply two 2-digit numbers: 45 × 32 = | **1440** | `45 ENTER 32 ×` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Solar Battery Storage Bank
> *Scenario:* Electrical engineers calculate total energy cells across multi-tier battery racks.

- **Case Question 1:** A residential battery rack holds 24 rows of 6 lithium-iron cells. Decompose 24 into 20 + 4 on your Place Value Tray to find total cells:
  - *Expected Result:* **144 cells** | *Keystrokes:* `24 ENTER 6 ×`
- **Case Question 2:** A commercial microgrid bank has 32 modules with 15 supercapacitors each. Use a 2×2 area model to find the total capacitors:
  - *Expected Result:* **480 capacitors** | *Keystrokes:* `32 ENTER 15 ×`
- **Case Question 3:** Backup Rack Expansion: 7 inverter trays each hold 43 battery cells. Split 43 into 40 + 3 partial products to find the total cells:
  - *Expected Result:* **301 (280 + 21 = 301).** | *Keystrokes:* `43 ENTER 7 ×`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: To size a 25-row by 14-column solar array, an apprentice computed (20×10) + (5×4) = 220, forgetting the two cross-rectangles. Find the true cell count:
  - *Expected Result:* **True product is 350 ((20×14) + (5×14) = 280 + 70 = 350).** | *Keystrokes:* `25 ENTER 14 ×`

### Unit 05: Long Division, Quotients & Remainders
**Grades:** Grade 4 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (6×2 Whole Number Tray)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.4.NBT.B.6

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Divide 95 ÷ 6 on paper. State quotient and remainder, then verify on stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `15 R 5 (6 × 15 + 5 = 95).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `95 ENTER 6 ÷R`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** If 134 ÷ 5 = 26 R 4, verify by evaluating 26 × 5 + 4 on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `134 (Verified).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `26 ENTER 5 × 4 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | Verify division on stack: 25 × 3 + 1 = | **76** | `25 ENTER 3 × 1 +` |
| 3 | Divide 98 ÷ 4 on paper: state quotient and remainder = | **24 R 2** | `98 ENTER 4 ÷R` |
| 4 | Divide 125 ÷ 6 on paper: state quotient and remainder = | **20 R 5** | `125 ENTER 6 ÷R` |

- **Case Question 3:** Bulk Cement Hold: 146 export pallets are crane-lifted in slings of 6 pallets per lift. Find full lifts and leftover pallets:

### Unit 06: Fraction Equivalence & Common Denominators
**Grades:** Grade 4 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Fraction Studio (Comparator Well & Proportional Towers)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.4.NF.A.1, CCSS.MATH.4.NF.A.2

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Test 3/6 and 4/8 in the comparator well. What single simplified fraction do both equal?
  - *Verified Answer:* `1/2 (42.0 mm).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `. 3 . 6`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Find the common denominator and sum of 1/2 + 1/3 on paper, then check on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `5/6 (3/6 + 2/6 = 5/6).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 2 ENTER . 1 . 3 +`
- **Hands-On Block Focus (Fraction Multiplication & Division):**
  - *Multiplication:* Scaling one tower by another (e.g., 2/3 of 3/8 = 1/4 matching the 21 mm block).
  - *Division (Split & Rebuild 1 Full Tower):* To divide `1/2 ÷ 3/4`, split the 1/2 block (42 mm) into 3 equal 1/6 blocks (`÷ 3 -> 14 mm` is 1/4 of the mystery tower), then stack 4 of those 1/6 blocks (`× 4 -> 56 mm = 2/3`) to prove why `÷(3/4) = ×(4/3)`!

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Find equivalent fraction: 4/8 simplifies to = | **1/2** | `. 4 . 8` |
| 2 | Add unlike fractions: 1/3 + 1/6 = | **1/2** | `. 1 . 3 ENTER . 1 . 6 +` |
| 3 | Add unlike fractions: 1/4 + 3/8 = | **5/8** | `. 1 . 4 ENTER . 3 . 8 +` |
| 4 | Find common denominator and sum: 2/5 + 1/10 = | **1/2** | `. 2 . 5 ENTER . 1 . 1 0 +` |

| **1** | Core Fluency | Simplify to lowest terms: 6/8 = | 10 pts | **3/4** | `.6.8 ENTER` |
| **2** | Core Fluency | Simplify to lowest terms: 8/12 = | 10 pts | **2/3** | `.8.12 ENTER` |
| **3** | Core Fluency | Add related fractions: 1/3 + 1/6 = | 10 pts | **1/2** | `.1.3 ENTER .1.6 +` |
| **4** | Core Fluency | Add related fractions: 1/4 + 3/8 = | 10 pts | **5/8** | `.1.4 ENTER .3.8 +` |
| **5** | Core Fluency | Add unlike fractions: 1/2 + 1/3 = | 10 pts | **5/6** | `.1.2 ENTER .1.3 +` |
| **6** | Core Fluency | Add unlike fractions: 1/3 + 1/4 = | 10 pts | **7/12** | `.1.3 ENTER .1.4 +` |
| **7** | Application | Subtract unlike fractions: 3/4 − 1/3 = | 10 pts | **5/12** | `.3.4 ENTER .1.3 -` |
| **8** | Application | How many twelfths are equivalent to 2/3? | 10 pts | **8** | `.2.3 ENTER 12 ×` |
| **9** | Application | A stained-glass border combines 1/2 m, 1/4 m, and 1/8 m strips. Total length? | 10 pts | **7/8 m** | `.1.2 ENTER .1.4 + .1.8 +` |
| **10** | Error Analysis | Error Analysis: A student adds 1/2 + 1/3 = 2/5. Find the true sum: | 10 pts | **5/6** | `.1.2 ENTER .1.3 +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Stained Glass Window Assembly
> *Scenario:* Architectural glass artisans cut proportional strips and match lead-came joints using common denominators.

- **Case Question 1:** Cathedral Medallion Check: Two 1/4-meter ruby glass panels are soldered side-by-side. Compare them in the 84 mm well to simplify 2/4:
  - *Expected Result:* **1/2 (42.0 mm).** | *Keystrokes:* `.2.4 ENTER`
- **Case Question 2:** Lead-Came Border Joint: An artisan joins a 1/3-meter cobalt strip and a 1/4-meter amber strip. Trade for twelfths in the comparator well to find total length:
  - *Expected Result:* **7/12 (4/12 + 3/12 = 7/12).** | *Keystrokes:* `.1.3 ENTER .1.4 +`
- **Case Question 3:** Rose Window Arch: Combine a 1/2-meter central panel with a 1/6-meter border arch and simplify to lowest terms:
  - *Expected Result:* **2/3 (3/6 + 1/6 = 4/6 = 2/3).** | *Keystrokes:* `.1.2 ENTER .1.6 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: An apprentice glazier added 1/2 + 1/3 = 2/5 m of glass and cut the frame too short! Find the true combined length:
  - *Expected Result:* **5/6 (3/6 + 2/6 = 5/6, not 2/5).** | *Keystrokes:* `.1.2 ENTER .1.3 +`

### Unit 07: Prime Factorization, GCF/LCM & Factor Trees
**Grades:** Grade 4 – 5 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Prime Factor Studio (Logarithmic Prime Blocks 2, 3, 5, 7)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.4.OA.B.4, CCSS.MATH.6.NS.B.4

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Find the prime factorization of 18 and verify the product on your stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `18 (2 × 3 × 3 = 18).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `2 ENTER 3 × 3 ×`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Given GCF(8, 12) = 4, find LCM(8, 12) on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `24 ((8 × 12) ÷ 4 = 24).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 12 × 4 ÷`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Multiply prime factorization of 20: 2 × 2 × 5 = | **20** | `2 ENTER 2 × 5 ×` |
| 2 | Multiply prime factorization of 30: 2 × 3 × 5 = | **30** | `2 ENTER 3 × 5 ×` |
| 3 | Find the Greatest Common Factor (GCF) of 12 and 18: | **6** | `2 ENTER 3 ×` |
| 4 | Find the Least Common Multiple (LCM) of 6 and 8 given GCF(6, 8) = 2: | **24** | `6 ENTER 8 × 2 ÷` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Gear Train Planetary Mechanism
> *Scenario:* Mechanical engineers design clockwork gear trains using prime tooth counts (2, 3, 5, 7) so wear distributes evenly across every rotation cycle.

- **Case Question 1:** Pinion Tooth Decomposition: A 12-tooth brass pinion gear is machined for a clockwork train. Stack your Prime Factor blocks to build 12:
  - *Expected Result:* **2 × 2 × 3 = 12** | *Keystrokes:* `2 ENTER 2 × 3 ×`
- **Case Question 2:** Shared Gear Pitch (GCF): An 18-tooth drive gear meshes with a 24-tooth idler gear. Find the largest shared tooth grouping GCF(18, 24):
  - *Expected Result:* **6 (2 × 3 = 6).** | *Keystrokes:* `18 ENTER 24 GCF`
- **Case Question 3:** Full Mesh Alignment Cycle (LCM): A 12-tooth gear drives an 18-tooth gear. After how many tooth-meshes do the exact same two marked teeth meet again?
  - *Expected Result:* **36** | *Keystrokes:* `12 ENTER 18 LCM`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: An apprentice claims a 9-tooth cog has a prime number of teeth so it cannot share factors. Divide 9 ÷ 3 to prove 9 is composite:
  - *Expected Result:* **3 (9 = 3 × 3, composite).** | *Keystrokes:* `9 ENTER 3 ÷`

### Unit 08: The 4-Register Stack: Four Operations & Automatic Lift
**Grades:** Grade 4 – 5 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Expression Tree Diagram & 4-Register Stack
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.5.OA.A.1, CCSS.MATH.5.OA.A.2

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** A baker bakes 15 cookies, sells 6 of them, and packs the remaining cookies equally into 3 bags. How many cookies go in each bag?
  - *Verified Answer:* `3 cookies per bag ((15 - 6) ÷ 3 = 3).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `1 5 ENTER 6 - 3 ÷`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Evaluate (24 ÷ 6) + 15 by solving the division group first and then adding 15 to that result.
  - *Verified Answer:* `19 (24 ÷ 6 = 4, then 4 + 15 = 19).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `2 4 ENTER 6 ÷ 1 5 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Solve (14 - 5) × 3 by finding the difference inside the parentheses first, then multiplying by 3. | **27** | `1 4 ENTER 5 - 3 ×` |
| 2 | Solve 30 ÷ (2 + 3) by adding the divisor group (2 + 3) first, then dividing 30 by that sum. | **6** | `2 ENTER 3 + 3 0 x⇄y ÷` |
| 3 | Evaluate (9 + 7) ÷ (8 - 4) by finding the numerator sum and denominator difference first, then dividing. | **4** | `9 ENTER 7 + 8 ENTER 4 - ÷` |
| 4 | A farmer collects 20 eggs, sets aside 2 cracked eggs, and divides the rest equally among 6 cartons. How many per carton? | **3 eggs** | `2 0 ENTER 2 - 6 ÷` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Space Elevator Cargo Transfer
> *Scenario:* Astrodynamicists balance orbital payload pods on a 4-Register Stack Stage without parentheses.

- **Case Question 1:** Pod A carries 15 tons and transfers 6 tons to the station; the remaining cargo is split across 3 shuttles: (15 − 6) ÷ 3 =
  - *Expected Result:* **3** | *Keystrokes:* `15 ENTER 6 - 3 ÷`
- **Case Question 2:** Two thruster banks fire together: (8 + 2) pods each burning (9 − 4) kg of fuel. Evaluate (8 + 2) × (9 − 4):
  - *Expected Result:* **50** | *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 2 + 9 ENTER 4 - ×`
- **Case Question 3:** From a 100-ton counterweight reserve, subtract 4 racks of 5 tons plus a 10-ton tether clamp: 100 − (4 × 5 + 10) =
  - *Expected Result:* **70** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 5 × 10 + 100 x⇄y -`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student executes left-to-right on 10 + 2 × 5 and gets 60.
  - *Expected Result:* **Multiplication comes first: 2 × 5 = 10; 10 + 10 = 20.** | *Keystrokes:* `2 ENTER 5 × 10 +`

### Unit 09: Adding & Subtracting Fractions & Mixed Numbers
**Grades:** Grade 5 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Fraction Studio (84 mm Proportional Towers)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.5.NF.A.1, CCSS.MATH.5.NF.A.2

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Add 1 3/4 + 2 1/8 on paper and verify on your calculator.
  - *Verified Answer:* `3 7/8 (1 6/8 + 2 1/8 = 3 7/8).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `1 . 3 . 4 ENTER 2 . 1 . 8 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Solve 4 1/3 − 2 1/2 on paper with regrouping and check on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `1 5/6 (3 8/6 − 2 3/6 = 1 5/6).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `4 . 1 . 3 ENTER 2 . 1 . 2 -`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Add mixed numbers: 1 1/2 + 2 1/4 = | **3 3/4** | `1 . 1 . 2 ENTER 2 . 1 . 4 +` |
| 2 | Subtract mixed numbers: 3 1/2 − 1 1/4 = | **2 1/4** | `3 . 1 . 2 ENTER 1 . 1 . 4 -` |
| 3 | Add mixed numbers with unlike denominators: 2 1/3 + 1 1/6 = | **3 1/2** | `2 . 1 . 3 ENTER 1 . 1 . 6 +` |
| 4 | Subtract with regrouping: 4 1/4 − 1 3/4 = | **2 1/2** | `4 . 1 . 4 ENTER 1 . 3 . 4 -` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Chef's Artisan Bread Recipe
> *Scenario:* A master baker combines sourdough flours across multiple dough troughs.

- **Case Question 1:** Multi-Grain Trough Blend: The baker blends 2 1/3 cups of dark rye flour with 1 1/2 cups of whole wheat flour. Find the combined flour volume:
  - *Expected Result:* **3 5/6 cups** | *Keystrokes:* `2 . 1 . 3 ENTER 1 . 1 . 2 +`
- **Case Question 2:** Levain Starter Portioning: A proofing tub holds 3 1/4 cups of sourdough starter. After scooping out 1 1/2 cups for baguettes, how much starter remains?
  - *Expected Result:* **1 3/4 cups** | *Keystrokes:* `3 . 1 . 4 ENTER 1 . 1 . 2 -`
- **Case Question 3:** Brioche Hydration Mix: Combine 1 3/8 cups of warm buttermilk and 2 1/4 cups of whole milk in the mixer bowl. What is the total liquid volume?
  - *Expected Result:* **3 5/8 cups** | *Keystrokes:* `1 . 3 . 8 ENTER 2 . 1 . 4 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Subtracting 1 1/2 cups of flour from 3 1/4 cups, an apprentice subtracted 1/2 − 1/4 backward and wrote 2 1/4 cups. Trade 1 Whole for 4/4 to fix it:
  - *Expected Result:* **1 3/4 (must unbundle 1 whole to make 2 5/4 − 1 2/4 = 1 3/4).** | *Keystrokes:* `3 . 1 . 4 ENTER 1 . 1 . 2 -`

### Unit 10: Fraction Studio: Proportions, Multiplication & Tower Division
**Grades:** Grade 5 | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-6 | **Visual / Block Model:** Fraction Studio (84 mm Proportional Towers)
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.5.NF.B.4, CCSS.MATH.5.NF.B.7

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Find 2/3 of 3/4 by multiplying (2/3) × (3/4) and simplifying to lowest terms.
  - *Verified Answer:* `1/2 (6/12 = 1/2, 42.0 mm).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `.2.3 ENTER .3.4 ×`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Use Split & Rebuild to evaluate (3/4) ÷ (3/8) on paper and check on your SC-6.
  - *Verified Answer:* `2 ((3/4) × (8/3) = 2 wholes).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `.3.4 ENTER .3.8 ÷`
- **Hands-On Block Focus (Fraction Multiplication & Division):**
  - *Multiplication:* Scaling one tower by another (e.g., 2/3 of 3/8 = 1/4 matching the 21 mm block).
  - *Division (Split & Rebuild 1 Full Tower):* To divide `1/2 ÷ 3/4`, split the 1/2 block (42 mm) into 3 equal 1/6 blocks (`÷ 3 -> 14 mm` is 1/4 of the mystery tower), then stack 4 of those 1/6 blocks (`× 4 -> 56 mm = 2/3`) to prove why `÷(3/4) = ×(4/3)`!

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Multiply fraction scaling: 1/2 × 3/4 = | **3/8** | `.1.2 ENTER .3.4 ×` |
| 2 | Multiply fractions and simplify: 2/3 × 3/8 = | **1/4** | `.2.3 ENTER .3.8 ×` |
| 3 | Divide by a whole number: 1/2 ÷ 3 = | **1/6** | `.1.2 ENTER 3 ÷` |
| 4 | Divide by a fraction (Split & Rebuild): 1/2 ÷ 3/4 = | **2/3** | `.1.2 ENTER .3.4 ÷` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Suspension Bridge Tower Cable Geometry
> *Scenario:* Civil engineers calculate tower tension ratios using fraction multiplication and division.

- **Case Question 1:** Secondary Suspender Scaling: A main bridge cable carries 3/4 of a kilonewton unit load, and a secondary suspender carries 1/2 of that load (1/2 × 3/4). Find the suspender load:
  - *Expected Result:* **3/8 meter (31.5 mm).** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 2 ENTER . 3 . 4 ×`
- **Case Question 2:** Three-Strand Load Sharing: A 1/2-meter anchor rod is split equally among 3 steel tie-down brackets (1/2 ÷ 3). What fraction of a meter does each bracket hold?
  - *Expected Result:* **1/6 (14.0 mm).** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 2 ENTER 3 ÷`
- **Case Question 3:** Full Tower Reconstruction: A 1/2-meter scale test cable represents 3/4 of the design span. Use Split & Rebuild (1/2 ÷ 3/4) to find the full 1-tower design span:
  - *Expected Result:* **2/3 (56.0 mm tower).** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 2 ENTER . 3 . 4 ÷`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: A structural intern computed 1/2 ÷ 3/4 = 3/8 by multiplying instead of dividing. Use the 84 mm Split & Rebuild model to explain why 1/2 ÷ 3/4 = 2/3:
  - *Expected Result:* **Dividing by 3/4 scales up by 4/3: 1/2 × 4/3 = 2/3 (56 mm).** | *Keystrokes:* `. 1 . 2 ENTER . 3 . 4 ÷`

## 📕 Book 4: High School & STEM Lab (Grades 9–12 & AP • SC-32)
*Covers Units 01 through 10 plus 4 Cumulative Benchmark & Capstone Mastery Exams: Linear Functions & Slope, Factoring Quadratics, Quadratic Formula & [POLY], Higher-Degree Polynomials & Horner's Method, Compound Growth & Exponents, Logarithms & Base e, Right-Triangle Trig, 2D Polar Vectors, Combinatorics & Statistics, and Binary/Hex Digital Logic (840 active problems).*

### Unit 01: Linear Equations, Rate of Change & Slope Pipelines
**Grades:** Grade 9 – 10 (Algebra 1) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Coordinate Slope Triangle Diagram & 4-Level Stack
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSA.CED.A.2, CCSS.MATH.HSF.IF.B.6

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Find the slope passing through (1, 4) and (5, 16) on paper, then check on SC-32.
  - *Verified Answer:* `3 (12 ÷ 4 = 3).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `16 ENTER 4 - 5 ENTER 1 - ÷`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Given line with m = 3 through (2, 4), find y when x = 8.
  - *Verified Answer:* `22 (3(8−2) + 4 = 18 + 4 = 22).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 2 - 3 × 4 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Find slope between (3, 5) and (7, 17): m = | **3** | `17 ENTER 5 - 7 ENTER 3 - ÷` |
| 2 | Find slope between (-2, 8) and (4, -4): m = | **-2** | `-4 ENTER 8 - 4 ENTER -2 - ÷` |
| 3 | Line has m = 4 through (1, 3). Find y when x = 6: y = | **23** | `6 ENTER 1 - 4 × 3 +` |
| 4 | Find the x-intercept of 3x + 6y = 24: x = | **8** | `24 ENTER 3 ÷` |

### Unit 02: Algebraic Factoring, Area Models & Quadratic Trinomials
**Grades:** Grade 9 – 10 (Algebra 1) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Prime Factor Studio (Factor Pairs) & 2×2 Algebraic Area Grid
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSA.SSE.B.3, CCSS.MATH.HSA.REI.B.4

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Factor x² + 7x + 12 by decomposing 12 with Prime Factor blocks to find the factor pair whose sum is 7, and find both roots of x² + 7x + 12 = 0.
  - *Verified Answer:* `3 × 4 = 12 and 3 + 4 = 7; factored form (x + 3)(x + 4), roots x = -3 and x = -4.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `POLY 1 B 7 C 1 2 D XEQ`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Factor x² - 5x + 6 into two binomials by finding two negative factors of +6 whose sum is -5, and state both roots.
  - *Verified Answer:* `(-2) × (-3) = +6 and (-2) + (-3) = -5; factored form (x - 2)(x - 3), roots x = 2 and x = 3.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `POLY 1 B 5 +/- C 6 D XEQ`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Factor x² + 6x + 8 by decomposing 8 into a factor pair that sums to 6, and find the roots. | **(x + 2)(x + 4); roots -2, -4** | `POLY 1 B 6 C 8 D XEQ` |
| 2 | Factor x² - 4 (difference of two squares, where the linear coefficient b = 0). | **(x - 2)(x + 2); roots 2, -2** | `POLY 1 B 0 C 4 +/- D XEQ` |
| 3 | Factor x² + 5x + 4 into two binomials and state the solutions to x² + 5x + 4 = 0. | **(x + 1)(x + 4); roots -1, -4** | `POLY 1 B 5 C 4 D XEQ` |
| 4 | Factor x² - 5x + 6 using signed factor pairs and state both roots. | **(x - 2)(x - 3); roots 2, 3** | `POLY 1 B 5 +/- C 6 D XEQ` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Renaissance Garden Quadrangle Proportions
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 6 & Ch. 7: Renaissance architects proportioned walled courtyards using 2×2 algebraic area grids (x + p)(x + q) and Prime Factor blocks.

- **Case Question 1:** Main Cloister Lawn: Factor the courtyard area x² + 5x + 6 on the 2×2 area grid and verify the corner constant product:
  - *Expected Result:* **(x + 2)(x + 3) (2×3 = 6)** | *Keystrokes:* `2 ENTER 3 ×`
- **Case Question 2:** Fountain Terrace Cutout: Factor the difference-of-squares plaza x² − 16 and verify the corner square area:
  - *Expected Result:* **(x − 4)(x + 4) (4² = 16)** | *Keystrokes:* `4 x²`
- **Case Question 3:** Pavilion Stone Count: Evaluate the expanded courtyard area (x + 3)(x + 4) when base module x = 5 m:
  - *Expected Result:* **72 (8 × 9 = 72).** | *Keystrokes:* `5 ENTER 3 + 5 ENTER 4 + ×`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student claims (x + 3)² = x² + 9 at x = 2 (getting 13). Find true (2 + 3)²:
  - *Expected Result:* **25 (x² + 6x + 9 = 4 + 12 + 9 = 25).** | *Keystrokes:* `2 ENTER 3 + x²`

### Unit 03: The Quadratic Formula & Automatic [POLY] Root Extraction
**Grades:** Grade 9 – 11 (Algebra 1 & 2) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Parabola Root Diagram & SC-32 [POLY] Solver
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSA.REI.B.4.B, CCSS.MATH.HSN.CN.C.7

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Evaluate the discriminant for 2x² − 7x + 3 = 0 on paper and verify on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `25 (49 − 24 = 25; 2 real rational roots).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `-7 x² 4 ENTER 2 × 3 × -`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Use [POLY] on the SC-32 to solve 2x² − 5x − 3 = 0.
  - *Verified Answer:* `x1 = 3.0, x2 = -0.5.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `POLY 2 B -5 C -3 D XEQ`

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Trajectory Ballistics Flight Arc Analysis
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 7: Aerospace guidance engineers analyze parabolic flight equations on the 4-level stack using [POLY] and [SOLVE] to find real crossings and impact times.

- **Case Question 1:** Radar Target Clearance: Evaluate discriminant Δ = b² − 4ac for flight clearance curve 2x² − 7x + 3 = 0:
  - *Expected Result:* **25 (2 real roots)** | *Keystrokes:* `-7 x² 4 ENTER 2 × 3 × -`
- **Case Question 2:** Wind-Tunnel Calibration Arc: Extract the reference roots of x² + 5x + 6 = 0 using [POLY]:
  - *Expected Result:* **x1 = -2, x2 = -3** | *Keystrokes:* `POLY 1 B 5 C 6 D XEQ`
- **Case Question 3:** Downrange Impact Solution: Extract the roots of trajectory profile 2x² − 5x − 3 = 0 using [POLY]:
  - *Expected Result:* **x1 = 3.0, x2 = -0.5** | *Keystrokes:* `POLY 2 B -5 C -3 D XEQ`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student says Δ < 0 for x² + 4 = 0 means no roots. Evaluate Δ:
  - *Expected Result:* **-16 (2 complex conjugate roots ±2i).** | *Keystrokes:* `0 x² 4 ENTER 1 × 4 × -`

### Unit 04: Polynomial Operations, Horner's Rule & Synthetic Division
**Grades:** Grade 10 – 11 (Algebra 2) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Nested Polynomial Pipeline Diagram & SC-32 Stack
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSA.APR.A.1, CCSS.MATH.HSA.APR.B.2

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Evaluate P(x) = 3x³ − 2x² + 4x − 8 at x = 2 using Horner's rule on the stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `16 (((3(2)−2)(2)+4)(2)−8 = (4(2)+4)(2)−8 = 12(2)−8 = 16).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `3 ENTER 2 × -2 + 2 × 4 + 2 × -8 +`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Test if (x − 3) is a factor of P(x) = x³ − 4x² + x + 6 using synthetic division on stack.
  - *Verified Answer:* `Remainder = 0; (x − 3) is a factor.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `1 ENTER 3 × -4 + 3 × 1 + 3 × 6 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

| 1 | Evaluate P(x) = x² − 6x + 8 at x = 4: P(4) = | **0** | `1 ENTER 4 × -6 + 4 × 8 +` |
| 2 | Evaluate P(x) = 2x³ + 3x² − 5x + 1 at x = 2: P(2) = | **19** | `2 ENTER 2 × 3 + 2 × -5 + 2 × 1 +` |
| 3 | Find remainder of (x³ + 2x² − 7x − 2) ÷ (x − 2): R = | **0** | `1 ENTER 2 × 2 + 2 × -7 + 2 × -2 +` |
| 4 | Evaluate P(x) = 4x² − 12x + 9 at x = 1.5: P(1.5) = | **0** | `4 ENTER 1.5 × -12 + 1.5 × 9 +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Signal Processing Filter Transfer Functions
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 7, Ch. 8 & Ch. 14: Audio engineers evaluate high-order filter transfer polynomials on the stack using Horner's nested pipeline.

- **Case Question 1:** Stage-1 Gain Response: Evaluate filter polynomial P(x) = 2x² − 4x + 5 at frequency ratio x = 3 via Horner's rule:
  - *Expected Result:* **11** | *Keystrokes:* `2 ENTER 3 × -4 + 3 × 5 +`
- **Case Question 2:** Notch Filter Null Check: Evaluate cubic transfer function P(x) = x³ − 4x² + x + 6 at x = 2 via Horner's rule:
  - *Expected Result:* **0 ((x − 2) is a factor)** | *Keystrokes:* `1 ENTER 2 × -4 + 2 × 1 + 2 × 6 +`
- **Case Question 3:** Remainder Theorem Telemetry: Evaluate filter polynomial P(x) = 2x² − 3x + 5 at x = 3 to find the remainder when dividing P(x) by (x − 3):
  - *Expected Result:* **Remainder = 14 (Remainder Theorem)** | *Keystrokes:* `2 ENTER 3 × -3 + 3 × 5 +`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student omits the 0x² coefficient in P(x) = x³ − 7x + 6 at x = 2. Evaluate P(2) with the 0x² term included:
  - *Expected Result:* **0 (Missing degree terms must have coefficient 0 in synthetic division: 1, 0, -7, 6)** | *Keystrokes:* `1 ENTER 2 × 0 + 2 × -7 + 2 × 6 +`

### Unit 05: Exponential Growth, Decay & Continuous Compounding
**Grades:** Grade 10 – 12 (Algebra 2 & Precalculus) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Prime Factor Studio & SC-32 Scientific Stack ([yˣ], [eˣ])
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSF.LE.A.1, CCSS.MATH.HSF.LE.A.4

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** A bacteria culture starts with P = 500 cells and triples every hour. Calculate the population after t = 4 hours.
  - *Verified Answer:* `3^4 = 81; 500 × 81 = 40,500 bacteria.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `3 ENTER 4 yˣ 5 0 0 ×`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** A bacterial colony of 500 cells grows continuously at rate r = 0.12 per hour. Find population at t = 8 hrs.
  - *Verified Answer:* `1,305.85 cells (500 × e^0.96 ≈ 1,305.85).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `0.12 ENTER 8 × eˣ 500 ×`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Invest $2,000 at 6% compounded annually for 5 years: A = | **$2,676.45** | `2000 ENTER 1.06 ENTER 5 yˣ ×` |
| 2 | Continuous compounding: $5,000 at 4% for 8 years: A = | **$6,885.64** | `0.04 ENTER 8 × eˣ 5000 ×` |
| 3 | Depreciation: Car drops 15% per year from $30,000 after 4 years: A = | **$15,660.19** | `30000 ENTER 0.85 ENTER 4 yˣ ×` |
| 4 | Half-life: 100g substance with half-life 6 days after 18 days: A = | **12.5g** | `100 ENTER 0.5 ENTER 3 yˣ ×` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Global Fiber Optic Bandwidth Expansion
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 3 & Ch. 11: Network architects and financial engineers model compound traffic growth, TVM cash flows, and optical attenuation using exponential functions.

- **Case Question 1:** Discrete Backbone Expansion: A 1,000 Gbps node grows 5% annually for 10 years. Find the capacity A = 1000(1.05)¹⁰:
  - *Expected Result:* **$1,628.89** | *Keystrokes:* `1000 ENTER 1.05 ENTER 10 yˣ ×`
- **Case Question 2:** Continuous Traffic Surge: Model 1,000 units growing continuously at 5% for 10 years (A = Peʳᵗ):
  - *Expected Result:* **$1,648.72** | *Keystrokes:* `0.05 ENTER 10 × eˣ 1000 ×`
- **Case Question 3:** Optical Laser Attenuation: An 80 mW signal halves every 50 km. Find the power after 3 half-distances:
  - *Expected Result:* **10 mW** | *Keystrokes:* `80 ENTER 0.5 ENTER 3 yˣ ×`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student enters rate 5% as 5 instead of 0.05. Convert 5% to its decimal rate on the stack:
  - *Expected Result:* **0.05 (5% = 5/100 = 0.05; using 5 multiplies by 6 instead of 1.05!)** | *Keystrokes:* `5 ENTER 100 ÷`

### Unit 06: Natural Logarithms, Inverse Exponentials & Doubling Time [LN]
**Grades:** Grade 11 – 12 (Algebra 2 & Precalc) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Prime Factor Studio (Logarithmic Height Towers) & SC-32 [LN]
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSF.BF.B.5, CCSS.MATH.HSF.LE.A.4

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Find the exact doubling time for a population growing at 4% continuously.
  - *Verified Answer:* `17.33 years (ln(2) ÷ 0.04 ≈ 17.329).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `2 LN 0.04 ÷`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Evaluate log_2(64) on the stack using natural logarithms.
  - *Verified Answer:* `6 (2⁶ = 64).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `64 LN 2 LN ÷`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Find the doubling time at 8% continuous interest: t = | **8.66 years** | `2 LN 0.08 ÷` |
| 2 | Find the tripling time at 5% continuous growth: t = | **21.97 years** | `3 LN 0.05 ÷` |
| 3 | Evaluate log_3(81) using change of base: | **4** | `81 LN 3 LN ÷` |
| 4 | Solve e^(0.03 t) = 5 for t: t = | **53.65** | `5 LN 0.03 ÷` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Carbon-14 Archeological Dating Laboratory
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 1 & Ch. 3: Geochemists calibrate spectrometer ratios and Willard Libby Carbon-14 decay constants using natural and base-10 logarithms.

- **Case Question 1:** Isotope Benchmark Rate: Find the continuous doubling/halving time t = ln(2) ÷ 0.07 for a 7% rate:
  - *Expected Result:* **9.90 years** | *Keystrokes:* `2 LN 0.07 ÷`
- **Case Question 2:** Decade Ratio Calibration: Evaluate the natural logarithm of a 10× isotope ratio, ln(10):
  - *Expected Result:* **2.3026** | *Keystrokes:* `10 LN`
- **Case Question 3:** Spectrometer Base Conversion: Evaluate log₁₀(1000) using the natural log change-of-base pipeline ln(1000) ÷ ln(10):
  - *Expected Result:* **3.0** | *Keystrokes:* `1000 LN 10 LN ÷`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student tries to calculate ln(-4). Explain the error.
  - *Expected Result:* **Logarithms of negative numbers are undefined in real numbers (eˣ > 0 always).** | *Keystrokes:* `4 LN`

### Unit 07: Right-Triangle Trigonometry, Unit Circles & Waveforms
**Grades:** Grade 10 – 12 (Geometry & Precalculus) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Right-Triangle & Unit-Circle Diagram & SC-32 Trig Keys
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSG.SRT.C.6, CCSS.MATH.HSG.SRT.C.8

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** A 24 m guy wire makes a 30° angle with the ground. Find the tower height.
  - *Verified Answer:* `12.0 m (24 × 0.5 = 12.0 m).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `24 ENTER 30 SIN ×`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** A flagpole 12 m tall casts a 9 m shadow. Find the sun's angle of elevation.
  - *Verified Answer:* `53.13° (atan(12/9) ≈ 53.13°).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `12 ENTER 9 ÷ ATAN`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Find opposite leg: hyp = 20, θ = 30°: opp = | **10.0** | `20 ENTER 30 SIN ×` |
| 2 | Find adjacent leg: hyp = 50, θ = 45°: adj = | **35.36** | `50 ENTER 45 COS ×` |
| 3 | Find angle θ where opp = 5, adj = 12: θ = | **22.62°** | `5 ENTER 12 ÷ ATAN` |
| 4 | Evaluate tan(60°): tan(60°) = | **1.7321** | `60 TAN` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Geodetic Mountain Peak Triangulation
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 4 & Ch. 6: Topographical surveyors triangulate summit elevations using Eratosthenes sightlines and right-triangle trigonometry.

- **Case Question 1:** Laser Sightline Rise: A 10 m calibration mast is sighted at θ = 30°. Find the vertical rise opp = 10·sin(30°):
  - *Expected Result:* **5.0 m** | *Keystrokes:* `10 ENTER 30 SIN ×`
- **Case Question 2:** Ridge Slope Angle: A ridge rises 3 m over a 4 m horizontal run. Find the angle of elevation θ = atan(3 ÷ 4):
  - *Expected Result:* **36.87°** | *Keystrokes:* `3 ENTER 4 ÷ ATAN`
- **Case Question 3:** Direct Line-of-Sight Distance: Survey markers stand 5 km East and 12 km North. Find the hypotenuse distance:
  - *Expected Result:* **13.0** | *Keystrokes:* `5 x² 12 x² + sqrt`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Calculator in Radian mode gives sin(30) = -0.988.
  - *Expected Result:* **Switch mode to Degrees (DEG annunciator) so 30° yields sin(30°) = 0.5.** | *Keystrokes:* `30 SIN`

### Unit 08: 2D Vectors, Polar Conversions & Resultant Forces
**Grades:** Grade 11 – 12 (Precalc & Physics) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** 2D Vector Right-Triangle Diagram & SC-32 [▸θ,r]
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSN.VM.A.1, CCSS.MATH.HSN.VM.A.3

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** A surveyor walks 60 m East (x = 60) and 80 m North (y = 80). Find straight-line distance r and angle θ.
  - *Verified Answer:* `r = 100 m, θ = 53.13°.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `80 ENTER 60 ▸θ,r`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Add vectors A = <6, 8> and B = <2, 7>. Find resultant magnitude and angle.
  - *Verified Answer:* `r = 17.0, θ = 61.93° (<8, 15> -> 8²+15²=289, √289=17).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `8 ENTER 7 + 6 ENTER 2 + ▸θ,r`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Convert <5, 12> to polar: r = | **13.0** | `12 ENTER 5 ▸θ,r` |
| 2 | Find heading angle for <5, 12>: θ = | **67.38°** | `12 ENTER 5 ▸θ,r x⇄y` |
| 3 | Convert <8, 6> to polar: r = | **10.0** | `6 ENTER 8 ▸θ,r` |
| 4 | Add vectors <4, 3> and <8, 6>. Find magnitude r = | **15.0** | `3 ENTER 6 + 4 ENTER 8 + ▸θ,r` |

> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 4 & Ch. 5: Flight navigators and electrical engineers compute wind triangles and Steinmetz AC phasors using 2D vector resolution and [▸θ,r].

- **Case Question 1:** Drone Test Flight: An aircraft drifts 3 km East (X) and 4 km North (Y). Convert <3, 4> to polar [▸θ,r]:
  - *Expected Result:* **r = 5.0, θ = 53.13°** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 3 ▸θ,r`
- **Case Question 2:** Jet Crosswind Leg: A jet travels 5 km East (X) and 12 km North (Y). Convert <5, 12> to polar [▸θ,r]:
  - *Expected Result:* **r = 13.0, θ = 67.38°** | *Keystrokes:* `12 ENTER 5 ▸θ,r`
- **Case Question 3:** Combined Thrust & Wind: Add force vectors <10, 15> and <20, 25> and find the resultant magnitude r:
  - *Expected Result:* **50.0 N (θ = 53.13°)** | *Keystrokes:* `40 ENTER 30 ▸θ,r`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student adds magnitudes 3 + 4 = 7 instead of vector sum 5.
  - *Expected Result:* **Vector magnitude uses Pythagorean theorem: √(3² + 4²) = 5, not 7.** | *Keystrokes:* `4 ENTER 3 ▸θ,r`

### Unit 09: Combinatorics, Probability Ratios & Series Accumulation
**Grades:** Grade 11 – 12 & AP (Precalc & AP Stats) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Decision Slot Diagram, Statistical Plot & SC-32 [PROB] ([Pn,r]/[Cn,r])/[Σ+]
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSS.CP.B.9

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** A lottery draws 4 winning numbers from a pool of 10 (order does not matter). How many winning combinations are possible?
  - *Verified Answer:* `(10 × 9 × 8 × 7) / (4 × 3 × 2 × 1) = 5040 / 24 = 210 combinations.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `1 0 x! 4 x! ÷ 6 x! ÷`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Given the four data points {12, 15, 18, 21}, find the sample size n, the total sum of the values, and the sample mean x̄.
  - *Verified Answer:* `Count n = 4, Sum = 66, Mean x̄ = 66 / 4 = 16.5.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `12 Σ+ 15 Σ+ 18 Σ+ 21 Σ+ x̄,ȳ`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Evaluate 6! (the number of ways to arrange 6 distinct books on a shelf). | **6 × 5 × 4 × 3 × 2 × 1 = 720** | `6 x!` |
| 2 | How many ways can 5 runners finish in 1st, 2nd, and 3rd place? | **5 × 4 × 3 = 60** | `5 x! 2 x! ÷` |
| 3 | How many 2-person partnerships can be formed from 7 students? | **(7 × 6) / 2 = 21** | `7 x! 2 x! ÷ 5 x! ÷` |
| 4 | Find the mean x̄ of the two data values 5 and 15. | **(5 + 15) / 2 = 10** | `5 Σ+ 1 5 Σ+ x̄,ȳ` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Cyber Security Password Entropy Cryptography
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 9 & Ch. 10: Security engineers and statisticians calculate key entropy, combinatorial search spaces, and small-sample distributions using [PROB] ([Pn,r], [Cn,r]) and [Σ+].

  - *Expected Result:* **720** | *Keystrokes:* `10 ENTER 3 PROB Pn,r`
- **Case Question 2:** Unordered Backup Keys: How many unordered 3-key subsets can be chosen from 10 master keys, C(10, 3)?
  - *Expected Result:* **120** | *Keystrokes:* `10 ENTER 3 PROB Cn,r`
- **Case Question 3:** Cryptographic Token Pool: How many 5-token subsets can be drawn from a 52-token hardware pool, C(52, 5)?
  - *Expected Result:* **2,598,960** | *Keystrokes:* `52 ENTER 5 PROB Cn,r`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student uses P(6, 2) = 30 for a 2-person committee from 6 people. Find true C(6, 2):

### Unit 10: Digital Logic, Base Conversions & Computer Architecture
**Grades:** Grade 9 – 12 & AP (AP CSP & CS) | **Instrument:** StackCalc SC-32 | **Visual / Block Model:** Place Value Studio (Binary [0]/[1] Bit Sockets) & SC-32 [BASE]
**Key Standards:** CCSS.MATH.HSN.RN.A.1, CSTA 3A-DA-07

#### 1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions
- **Warm-Up A (Core Lab):** Express decimal 255 as a sum of powers of 2 to write its 8-bit binary and 2-digit hexadecimal representations.
  - *Verified Answer:* `Binary: 11111111_2 (eight 1s); Hexadecimal: FF_16 (15 × 16 + 15 = 255).`
  - *Keystrokes:* `2 5 5 BASE`
- **Warm-Up B (Extension Lab):** Compare 12 and 3 column by column in 4-bit binary to find the result of 12 OR 3 in both binary and decimal.
  - *Verified Answer:* `1100_2 OR 0011_2 = 1111_2 = 15_10.`
  - *Keystrokes:* `1 2 ENTER 3 +`

#### 2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)
| # | Investigation Prompt | Expected Solution | Calculator Verification Keys |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Decompose decimal 42 into powers of 2 to write its 6-bit binary representation. | **32 + 8 + 2 -> 101010_2** | `4 2 BASE` |
| 2 | Convert hexadecimal 2A_16 to decimal using place value. | **32 + 10 = 42** | `2 ENTER 1 6 × 1 0 + BASE` |
| 3 | Evaluate the bitwise AND of 15 and 7 in binary and decimal. | **0111_2 = 7** | `1 5 ENTER 8 ÷R x⇄y` |
| 4 | Evaluate the bitwise XOR of 12 and 10 in binary and decimal. | **0110_2 = 6** | `4 ENTER 2 +` |

#### 5. STEM Lab Case Study: Microcontroller Firmware Register Masking
> *Scenario:* Companion to Vol. II, Ch. 1, Ch. 10 & Ch. 13: Firmware engineers configure GPIO pins on the RP2350 microcontroller using hexadecimal masks and Shannon bitwise AND, OR, and XOR.

- **Case Question 1:** Full Port Enable: Convert decimal 255 to hexadecimal and binary for an 8-pin GPIO port:
  - *Expected Result:* **0xFF = 255 (BIN: 11111111)** | *Keystrokes:* `255 ENTER BASE`
- **Case Question 2:** Alternating Pin Merge: Combine odd-pin mask 0xAA and even-pin mask 0x55 using bitwise OR:
  - *Expected Result:* **0xFF = 255 (11111111)** | *Keystrokes:* `170 ENTER 85 +`
- **Case Question 3:** Lower-Nybble Status Mask: Isolate the lower 4 status bits of 0xFF using bitwise AND with 0x0F:
  - *Expected Result:* **0x0F = 15 (00001111)** | *Keystrokes:* `255 ENTER 16 ÷R x⇄y`
- **Case Question 4:** Error Analysis: Student adds 0x0F + 1 as 0x10 and claims it is 10 decimal. Find 15 + 1 in decimal:
  - *Expected Result:* **16 (0x10 = 16 decimal).** | *Keystrokes:* `15 ENTER 1 +`

### Section 1: Section 1: Step Counting, Benchmarks & Sequences
**Grades:** Foundational to Advanced | **Focus:** Unit steps, skip-counting, 10/100 benchmarks, and arithmetic series.

#### 1. Warm-Up Drills (W1–W6)

| **W1** | 76 + 4 + 19 = | *Complete 80 first, then + 19* | **99** |
| **W2** | 150 - 25 - 25 = | *Combine 25 + 25 = 50, subtract from 150* | **100** |
| **W3** | 1 + 3 + 5 + 7 = | *4 consecutive odd numbers -> 4²* | **16** |
| **W4** | 10 + 15 + 20 + 25 + 30 = | *5 terms × middle term (20)* | **100** |
| **W5** | 1 + 2 + 3 + ... + 8 = | *Gauss rule: 8 × 9 ÷ 2* | **36** |
| **W6** | 2 + 4 + 6 + 8 + 10 = | *n(n + 1) for 5 even terms: 5 × 6* | **30** |

| **W5** | (17 × 6) - (13 × 6) = | *Factor out 6: (17 - 13) × 6* | **24** |
| **W6** | (45 × 12) ÷ 18 = | *Cancel 9: (5 × 12) ÷ 2* | **30** |

- **7D #14 ((25²-15²)÷(25-15)):** `(a² - b²)/(a - b) = a + b = 25 + 15 = 40`

### Section 8: Section 8: Algebra, Quadratics & Coordinate Geometry
**Grades:** Grades 6–10+ | **Focus:** Linear equations, slope, Pythagorean distance, and quadratic roots.

| **W2** | Product of roots of x² + 11x + 24 = 0 : | *Vieta's rule: c / a = 24 / 1* | **24** |
| **W3** | Vertex x-coord of y = x² - 8x + 19 : | *Axis of symmetry x = -b / (2a) = 8 / 2* | **4** |
| **W4** | Solve for x: x + y = 12, x - y = 6 : | *Average (12 + 6) ÷ 2* | **9** |
| **W5** | Hypotenuse for legs 7 and 24 = | *Pythagorean triple (7, 24, 25)* | **25** |
| **W6** | 3D diagonal √(2² + 3² + 6²) = | *√(4 + 9 + 36) = √49* | **7** |

|:---:|---|---|---|
| **W1** | Hex 2F₁₆ in decimal = | *2 × 16 + 15 = 32 + 15* | **47** |
| **W2** | 12 (1100₂) XOR 10 (1010₂) = | *Differing bits: 0110₂* | **6** |
| **W3** | Left shift: 7 << 3 = | *7 × 2³ = 7 × 8* | **56** |
| **W4** | Binary 10101100₂ in hexadecimal = | *Nibbles 1010 (A) and 1100 (C)* | **AC** |
| **W5** | Decimal 4095 (2¹² - 1) in Hex = | *Three 4-bit 1111 nibbles -> FFF* | **FFF** |
| **W6** | 8-bit Two's Complement of -1 (Hex) = | *256 - 1 = 255 = FF₁₆* | **FF** |

#### 3. Optimal Champion Pipelines
- **12C #15 (192 AND 240):** `192 = 128 + 64, both inside top mask 240 -> 192`
- **12D #9 (10101100₂ in Hex):** `1010₂ = A, 1100₂ = C -> AC`
- **12D #12 (4095 in Hex):** `2¹² - 1 = 12 ones = three F nibbles -> FFF`
- **12D #20 (170 XOR 85):** `Complementary bit masks 10101010₂ ^ 01010101₂ = 11111111₂ = 255`
